Category: Technology

Apple has introduced changes to how macOS handles login keychains in version 26.4 and later, requiring an additional entropy file to unlock keychains alongside the user password. This new requirement impacts how users back up and transfer their login keychains between Macs, particularly concerning system integrity protections. Users need to disable certain security features temporarily to copy the necessary files to a new device.

 - macOS 26.4 requires an entropy file to derive the cryptographic key for unlocking login keychains.
 - Copying login keychains between Macs necessitates temporarily disabling System Integrity Protection.

Source: http://derflounder.wordpress.com/?p=13516

Category: Other
Importance: 7
This year marks an unprecedented Atlantic hurricane season, with no hurricanes forming as of September, the typical peak month for hurricane activity. The phenomenon of El Niño is primarily responsible, disrupting weather patterns and causing above-normal activity in the eastern North Pacific instead. Historical records indicate that a season without a single hurricane is exceedingly rare, last occurring prior to satellite monitoring in 1914.

 - For the first time in over 30 years, no hurricane has developed in the Atlantic as of September.
 - El Niño is affecting hurricane formation by causing increased wind shear over the Atlantic.

Source: https://www.bbc.co.uk/weather/articles/cwgkvyyg8115o?at_medium=RSS&at_campaign=rss

---

 - The Atlantic hurricane season could end without any hurricanes for the first time since 1914.
 - El Niño is causing increased wind shear in the Atlantic, hindering storm development.

Shannon Burns, a former technology executive, is pursuing a £76 million claim against Gitpod after winning a disability discrimination case regarding her dismissal. The Birmingham Employment Tribunal ruled that Gitpod failed to provide reasonable adjustments for her disabilities, allowing her to move forward with her claim, which includes projected future earnings and other losses resulting from the discrimination. The tribunal also ordered Gitpod to disclose information related to its recent acquisition by OpenAI as part of the proceedings.

 - The case could set a precedent for employment compensation related to disability in the UK.

Category: Other
Importance: 8
A study conducted on rocket seeds that traveled to the International Space Station in 2015 revealed that while they remained viable after returning to Earth, they grew more slowly and experienced accelerated aging compared to seeds that did not go to space. This research is significant for future long-term space missions, as understanding how seeds react to space conditions is essential for sustaining human life on Mars and other planets.

 - Rocket seeds sent to the ISS in 2015 were tested by 600,000 UK children and found to have reduced growth rates.
 - The study provides valuable insights into how spaceflight affects seed biology, which is crucial for future human exploration of Mars.

Source: https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/science/space/in-2015-2kg-of-rocket-seeds-went-to-the-space-station-after-their-return-600000-uk-children-helped-test-them-and-researchers-found-they-grew-more-slowly-but-remained-viable/articleshow/134653097.cms

Tensions between Pakistan and Afghanistan have escalated, with accusations against the Afghan Taliban of harboring militant groups like the Tehrik-i-Taliban Pakistan (TTP).

 - Tensions are rising between Pakistan and Afghanistan over the alleged support of the Taliban for militant groups.

Category: Geopolitics
Importance: 5
Scientists are evaluating the impact of climate change on icebergs in Greenland and Antarctica, with a focus on how this relates to movie depictions. While human-driven climate change is causing polar ice to melt, most icebergs do not pose a significant threat to populated areas, and their calving events are often part of a natural cycle. The gradual and slow nature of these changes contrasts with the urgent narrative often found in media portrayals.

 - Icebergs breaking off from Greenland are usually not large enough to cause significant threats to populated areas.
 - Human-driven climate change is causing melting at the poles, but the process is gradual and often misunderstood.

Source: https://www.scientificamerican.com/article/could-a-giant-iceberg-really-break-off-greenland-scientists-check-tom-cruises-new-movie-digger/

---


Category: Geopolitics
Importance: 5
Recent concerns have been raised by scientists regarding the portrayal of climate change and iceberg calving in media, particularly with the release of the film 'Digger', which depicts a catastrophic scenario involving a giant iceberg breaking off Greenland. In reality, while icebergs do calve from glaciers, the events are usually slow and do not pose an immediate threat to populated areas, although they can disrupt maritime activities. Human-driven climate change is causing long-term melting of ice at the poles, which may contribute to rising global sea levels.

 - The film 'Digger' misrepresents the speed and impact of iceberg calving from Greenland.
 - Icebergs pose minimal risk to human populations but can affect maritime activities.
 - Climate change is contributing to the melting of polar ice, affecting global sea levels.

Source: https://www.scientificamerican.com/article/could-a-giant-iceberg-really-break-off-greenland-scientists-check-tom-cruises-new-movie-digger/
